**live proof on the stunt node** (disks 7/8/9; GT §18's procedure as a spec): attach through the API → `lvs` shows the cache, hits climb under a read loop; yank disk 9 live → the udev event uncaches within seconds, the reader loop recovers on its next pass, the warning card and notification appear, `GET /v1/ahr/<p>` then reports `cache.state: absent` and the pool `healthy`; the write-then-yank variant → `filesystem.readOnly` true, Remount restores writes; detach on a healthy cache → disk 9 reads `available`; re-attach on the returned disk succeeds (label wiped); Playwright over the Cache block, the verbs, the advisory, the failure card. Cross-check (§2 rule): TrueNAS exposes only ZFS's L2ARC/SLOG and no cache on non-ZFS paths; Synology's SSD cache defaults to read-only with the same "SSD is consumable" warning — our shape matches, minus writeback by ruling. **Result (2026-09-24/25; GT 7414b88; slice 1 a9e0e2f + fix batch e09f4df; slice 2 35ba001 + fix batch 84d4856; slice 3 UI 40c1714 + fix batch 5c912d3; Remount verb cdf917a; live proofs 2eea41ac + 35615f4):** `parsers/dmsetup.ts` + lvs cache fields; `ahr-cache-state.ts` (pure; `dmsetup status` is health on every read — `Fail`/`Error` = structural non-numeric first token; a cache target with no legible dm answer reads `failed`, never `absent`); `ahr-cache.ts` (attach with rollback, detach with leftover-slice reclaim, `uncacheAhrLv` = the ONE home destroy uses too, `dropMissingCachePv` guard = `--removemissing` only with every band PV present, `vgreduce` scoped to our slices, rollback failures logged); routes attach/detach (202, in-flight guard, expansion-intent gate, offline/failed 409s), `POST /:name/cache/event` (udev rung: socket auth `system:udev-cache-event`, the verdict is the daemon's own read, 200 when nothing to recover), `POST /:name/remount` (umount + mount — btrfs refuses `remount,rw` after an error; 409 doors; confirm with the open-handle sentence); `packaging/anas-cache.rules` + `anas-cache-event.sh`; boot rung branch (d) gated on one `lvs`; `mountedReadOnly` on the pool; `AHR_READONLY_CLAUSE` shared; failed auto-recovery notifies like the boot rung. UI: Cache block (absent / leftover / healthy with hits-misses-ratio, policy, blocks / failed = every read errors until detach), cache disks own bay group and slice-bar segment and stay out of the banded layout, Attach cache… (picker SSDs first + reclaimable slice, the advisory, the rotating sentence), Detach cache (plain `Ext.Msg.confirm` — the route is a 202 by design), Remount (gated on `mountedReadOnly`), reload grid + detail on success AND failure; SSD wear stays on the Disks tab (ruling). Harness gained a static guard: every `confirmAndRun` verb must target a confirm-gated route. Live proofs on the stunt node (disks 7/8/9): `ahr-cache-api.spec.ts` 4/4 ×2 (attach → hits climb → detach hands the disk back; destroy uncaches first; the cache disk yanked live → udev rule fires (GT-25: `ID_PART_ENTRY_NAME` on the partition REMOVE uevent) → uncached in 0.4 s, rung done in 0.85 s (GT-26) → notification → RO → Remount restores writes; boot rung recovers a cached pool with its cache PV missing before mount); `ahr-cache-ui.spec.ts` 3/3 ×2 (dialogs, refused attach, Remount appears only when read-only). Ground truth corrected on the way: a streaming WRITE does not meet a dead writethrough cache (a miss goes to the origin), the transaction COMMIT does — `sync` forces read-only in 20 ms (GT-27), so a read-mostly pool may stay read-write after losing its cache; `cache.devices` is `[]` right after a yank (nothing left to name). Defects only the executing lane caught: Detach had no confirm at all; a second Details click stacked a duplicate detail window with frozen counters. Side findings: `deploy-anas.sh` had to learn the udev pieces; the gateway unit's `Requires=` without `PartOf=` (fixed as a rider). GitHub #63 closes when 0.4.0 ships.
**Review fixes (2026-09-25; AHR-DESIGN §13 "Fix batch (review, 2026-09-25)"):** cache PVs are classified by the `<pool>-cache<n>` label (a hand-added PV in the VG is an advisory, never a pool disk on destroy's wipe list); new cache state `inactive` for a cached LV that is not active (no dmsetup read, no rung acts on it); a missing cache device (`[unknown]` under the band guard, now `cache.deviceMissing`) reads `failed` even while dm still prints healthy counters, and the udev event also accepts a label sweep with no slice left, covering the idle pull GT-19 did not measure; notifications say "missing" only on that evidence; `findCacheSlices` throws on a failed or unparsable read and detach reads it before any mutation; attach labels through `cachePartitionLabel()`. The idle-pull shape still needs its live run on the stunt node. **Post-review fix batch (2026-09-25; 666f3e1 opus, live proofs c716c58 GLM):** the 0.4.0 code review found the cache verdicts over-trusting: a hand-added VG PV was counted as a cache disk (and zapped by destroy), an inactive cached LV read `failed` and the boot rung uncached it, an idle SSD pull was answered 'nothing to recover' while dm-cache still showed healthy counters, and `findCacheSlices` failed open to `[]` so a detach could skip every cleanup step and report success. Now: cache PVs are label-scoped (a foreign PV is a pool advisory, never a pool disk, and survives destroy), `AhrCacheState` gains `inactive` (LV not active, SSD not blamed) with `deviceMissing` on the wire, the boot rung acts only on `failed` and words the notification by cause, the udev route also recovers on 'no slice left on any disk', and a failed slice read fails the step. Live proofs `ahr-cache-review.spec.ts` 3/3 ×2 on the stunt node: idle pull (no I/O after attach → yank → rung recovers before the first read succeeds; the transient `failed`+`deviceMissing:true` read caught once in four runs, the rung wins the race otherwise), band down with the SSD healthy (no uncache, no notification; `inactive` read with the device still named once a band is assembled and the LV deactivated), foreign PV (advisory names the PV, disk absent from `pool.disks`, cache size unchanged, destroy leaves the PV with no VG). Ground truth from the band-down shape: with a cached pool's ONLY band array stopped, `GET /v1/ahr/:name` answers 404 — pool discovery is mdstat-driven — so `inactive` is unreachable in that exact shape and the no-uncache guarantee holds because nothing sees the pool (§4 candidate).

**backup2.11** `[done 2026-09-25]` Backup source guard (design DESIGN.md "Source guard (backup2.11)"; the 2026-08 boot-race incident) — as a user, I want a backup whose source sits on a mount that is not there to fail loudly instead of backing up an empty directory as a good snapshot, so a boot race never costs me my last real backup. **Build:** `runBackup` step 0 (before `scanArchives`, any snapshot, any client call): `readSourceGuardFacts` once (`fstabPath` seam on `BackupRunDeps` as cloud has it), `unmountedMountFor` over every archive path, each hit rendered by `unmountedArchiveRefusal` (the archive-leading sentence DESIGN specifies; cloud's own sentence stays cloud's) (`pxar` and `img`), ALL refusals collected into one thrown error, joined on one line with `; ` so the unit's journal line — and the Last-run tooltip — carries every sentence, one sentence per archive naming the archive, path, mountpoint and fstab source; no other pre-flight changes (existence stays the client's). `BackupNestedScan.unmounted?: UnmountedMount` (shared `UnmountedMount` schema lifted next to the scan; additive, optional) filled by `preview-nested` from the same facts; `68-backup.js` renders the per-row alert (`anas-backup-arch-nested-alert`, the element that already carries the nested note): "Not mounted: <path> is on <mountpoint> (<source>), configured in /etc/fstab but not mounted. The run will be refused until it is mounted." — Save is not blocked. Run-failure surfaces unchanged: Last run tooltip = the sentence (via `failureDetailFromJournal`), `anas-backup` on-failure notification, dashboard `backup` warning. **Workflow:** *where:* nothing new — the wizard's archive rows, the Tasks grid's Last run cell, the notification, the dashboard. *Happy path:* a mounted source shows no alert and runs as before; a task defined while its mount is down shows the alert, saves, and runs once the mount is up. *Timeliness:* the alert refreshes with the existing scan (path pick / blur); the run reads the tables at run start, never a cached fact. *Failure as the user meets it:* red Last run with the sentence in the tooltip and the same sentence in the mail; recovery = mount it (Mounts menu or by hand) and Run now. **Acceptance:** runner tests over a mocked fstab + findmnt pair (one archive unmounted ⇒ the run throws before any `zfs snapshot`/`proxmox-backup-client` argv, sentence names all four facts; two unmounted archives ⇒ both sentences; an `img` archive on an unmounted mount ⇒ refused; unreadable tables ⇒ pass, fail-open; a mounted source ⇒ argv sequence byte-identical to today's tests); `preview-nested` route test (`unmounted` present with the mount named, absent otherwise, old-shape clients unaffected); harness entries for the alert render (unmounted / mounted / scan without the field). **Live proof on the stunt node** (`backup-source-guard.spec.ts`, repo `pbsgt` on the PBS VM; fixture gains a mountable `tmpfs … noauto` fstab line at `/mnt/gt-guardok` beside the unmountable CIFS line): task with an archive under the tmpfs line while unmounted → `preview-nested` carries `unmounted`, Run now → job failed with the sentence, `journalctl` shows the `anas-backup` notification (`notified via target`, zero `could not notify`), dashboard warning `backup`; `mount /mnt/gt-guardok` + a file → Run now completes and PBS lists the snapshot; the CIFS line → refused naming the CIFS source; Playwright: the row alert appears on the unmounted path and is absent on `/gtbackup`. Lane: opus (unit level while the node is held), spec run when the node frees. **Result (2026-09-24/25; unit slice 2fe03e9 + review fix batch b458f05 + live proof b0271bd):** `runBackup` step 0 = the ONE shared guard (`source-guard.ts`, `unmountedMountFor` + `unmountedArchiveRefusal`); on a refusal the run makes exactly one call (`findmnt`) and throws every offending archive's sentence joined on one journal line (`; `), so the Last-run tooltip carries them all; `pxar` and `img` alike; fail-open on unreadable tables; a mounted source's `proxmox-backup-client` argv is byte-identical to before (pinned). Shared `UnmountedMount` (`{mountpoint, source, fstype, disabled?}`) is the one shape the guard's facts and `preview-nested.unmounted` use; the wizard's per-row alert says the run will be refused until the mount is up, Save not blocked. Live proof `backup-source-guard.spec.ts` 5/5 ×2 on the stunt node (tmpfs `noauto` line + the CIFS line: refusal sentence with all four facts, `anas-backup` notification with zero render failures, `backup` warning in `/v1/status`, wizard alert on the guarded path and absent on `/gtbackup`, mount-then-run writes `guarded.pxar` to PBS); no product defect. The proof's own spec fixes (login before the item, `.anas-btn-backup-new`, per-keystroke path typing — the combobox commits per `change`, the grid row envelope) and one ground-truth find: a task re-created under a previously used name inherits its leftover systemd stamp and fires at once (SCHEDULES-GT-16…21; ruling pending) — the spec uses an absolute future date. Daemon 4535; harness 1620.

**disks.1** `[done 2026-09-25]` SMART readings age out (design pass 2026-09-24) — as a user, I want the Disks tab and the dashboard's disk warnings to reflect a disk's CURRENT SMART state, so a reallocated-sector count that starts climbing is seen within the hour, not at the next daemon restart. **Today:** `services/disk-identity-cache.ts` keeps one measured reading per disk for the daemon's lifetime (`isDue` is true only for never-read, standby or failed readings); probing rides `loadMany()` on the Disks list load (no poller, §2); both `smartctl` call sites pass `-n standby` (47a61c4). **TrueNAS cross-check (§2 rule):** smartd's default check interval is 30 minutes and TrueNAS's disk health follows it; TrueNAS also schedules SMART self-tests (short/long) and shows a disk's power state — the self-test schedule is NOT in this story (candidate: wire `smartd.conf`'s own `-s` directive, never a scheduler of ours), the power state is the rider below. **Mechanism:** a measured reading carries `measuredAt`; `isDue` also returns true when the reading is older than the re-probe cadence (default 30 min, `ANAS_SMART_REPROBE_MS` override for tests); the re-probe is the same `-n standby` call, so a sleeping disk is never woken and keeps its last measured values under the existing `stale: standby` mark (the mark gains the reading's age); a failed re-probe keeps the last measured reading under `probe-failed` with the existing backoff; at most one probe per disk per pass; `loadMany` is called from every consumer that reports health (Disks list, dashboard disk warnings — verify the dashboard path calls it, else route it through), so a warning surfaces within one cadence of the disk being awake and anyone pulling. No timer, no background probe — a node nobody looks at probes nothing. **Rider 1 — power mode row:** `parsers/smartctl.ts` reads `power_mode.name` from the ATA identity JSON when present — smartctl 7.5 emits `"power_mode": { "ata_value": <int>, "name": "<string>" }`, names drawn from `ACTIVE or IDLE`, `ACTIVE_NV_UP`, `ACTIVE_NV_DOWN`, `IDLE_A`, `IDLE_B`, `IDLE_C`, `STANDBY_Y` (the 2026-09-10 fleet capture recorded `255 "ACTIVE or IDLE"` and `129 "IDLE_A"`; SAS drives report no `power_mode` object at all — absent, never inferred); `Disk.powerMode?` (additive), reported verbatim, never mapped to a prettier word; the disk detail panel shows "Power mode: <name>" with "as of <age>" above the grid on BOTH SMART shapes (attribute table and summary), and not at all on the standby shape, where "spun down" is the current fact and the mode is only the last awake reading — display only, no policy (2026-09-10 ruling). **Rider 2 — version on the dashboard (#61 aside):** the dashboard header shows "ANAS <daemon version>", reusing the `x-anas-version` skew logic in `10-api.js`/`20-notinstalled.js` (skew keeps its banner; this is the plain label). ONE call, ONE source: `withInstallCheck` already probes `/v1/health` on every view render, so it stashes the version (`ANAS.daemonVersion`) and the label reads that — the dashboard makes no health call of its own. Hidden until it has a version, so an old gateway leaves no empty strip. **Workflow (§2 rule):** *Where:* Disks menu (grid + detail) and the dashboard; no new screen. *Happy path:* open Disks → readings older than 30 min are re-read on that load for awake disks; the grid's health column and the detail's attributes update; each detail shows "SMART read <age> ago"; a sleeping disk shows "last known — disk in standby, read <age> ago" (today's marker plus the age). *Timeliness:* a reading is at most one cadence old while someone pulls; the dashboard's disk warning appears on the pull that re-probed. *Failure:* a failed re-probe keeps the last values under the `probe-failed` marker with its reason (exists) and backs off (exists); nothing wakes a disk. *Proof:* unit tests over the cache with an injected clock (fresh → hit; aged → due; standby stderr fixture → not woken, age carried; failed → backoff unchanged; one probe per pass under concurrent loads); parser test for `power_mode` over both ATA fixtures (documented smartctl 7.5 shape carrying the 2026-09-10 fleet capture's values — `fixtures/system/NOTES.md` says so, and says to replace them with a verbatim capture when one is committed) and its absence on SAS JSON; **live proof on the stunt node** (virtual disks have no SMART — smartctl fails — so the cadence proof counts invocations, not values): with `ANAS_SMART_REPROBE_MS=2000` in a daemon drop-in, two Disks loads 3 s apart show two `smartctl` invocations per disk in the daemon journal, two loads 1 s apart show one; Playwright: the detail shows the read-age line and the power-mode row (absent on the virtual disks — assert absence, and presence from a fixture through the dev mock); the dashboard shows "ANAS <version>".**pvepool.1** `[done 2026-09-22]` Footprint ownership — daemon (GitHub #61; design DESIGN.md "PVE footprint ownership") — as a user with PVE guests on a pool, I want ANAS to manage datasets on that pool that are OUTSIDE PVE's footprint, so one pool can hold guests and NAS data. **Build:** `pveOwnership()` shared pure predicate + `PveOwnership` schema (`@anas/shared`), `SystemPoolFacts` (bootfs + `/` dataset) read once per request, `parsePveStorageCfg` keeps the configured path per ref (already does) and the daemon stops collapsing to the pool root; `PVE_GUEST_VOLUME_RE` corrected to `^(vm|base|subvol|basevol)-\d+-\S+$`; per-node `pve?` on `DatasetNode`/`Dataset` (additive); naming guard (400) on dataset create/rename/clone; consumers switched to the predicate: `routes/schedules.ts`, `replication-target.ts` + both replication routes, `iscsi-ownership.ts` + `iscsi-mutate.ts` (zvol and file backings), `backup-consistency.ts`, `routes/backup.ts` lun-sources; the four daemon `isPveManagedPool` copies deleted; pool-level refusals (`PUT /mountpoint`, export, destroy) unchanged + hard-refused on a system pool (no bypass). Refusal sentences name storage + dataset, never "this pool". **Acceptance:** unit table over a fixture set = {bare pool storage, nested `rpool/data` storage, `dir` storage on a dataset, system pool with bootfs, siblings at depth 1 and 2, all four guest prefixes, a `subvol-100-foo` and a `basevol-` case} for every consumer; **live proof (stunt node):** `pvesm add zfspool` on a loop pool + real `vm-100-disk-0`/`subvol-101-disk-0`/`basevol-102-disk-0` datasets + an ANAS sibling → schedule / replication target / add-LUN / backup snapshot succeed on the sibling and are refused on each owned kind with the named sentence; naming guard refused at the daemon; system-pool GT on a loop pool with `bootfs` set → destroy/export/change-mount hard-refused, `rpool`-shaped `ROOT/*` view-only, sibling manageable. Re-read the plugin on the target PVE version before dispatch (the regex and `-d1` are the contract). **Result:** shared `pveOwnership()` + `wouldBeClaimedByPve()` (`@anas/shared/pve-footprint`, 55 tests) → ONE daemon service `services/pve-footprint.ts` (`loadPveFootprint` once per request; boot facts from `zpool get bootfs` + `findmnt /`; `ownershipWithFallback` = the single fallback: unreadable boot facts ⇒ any pool with a zfspool storage reverts to the whole-pool rule — missing facts only tighten); per-node `pve` on the dataset tree; naming guard; owned datasets refused on create/props/destroy/snapshot create+rename+rollback+destroy/clone; schedules, replication target, iSCSI backings, backup consistency and the LUN picker all per-dataset; system pool = hard 409 `system-pool` on destroy/export/change-mount, confirm code does not bypass. Ten duplicate helpers deleted. Live proof `tests/integration/pvepool-api.spec.ts` (12 tests) over `test/stunt-node/pvepool-fixture.sh` (loop pools `pvfix` + `sysfix`, registered with `pvesm add`): 12/12 on the final build. The live proof caught one gap unit tests passed — snapshot create on the storage root was accepted (fixed, then the rename/rollback/destroy seams closed). Side finding: four older integration specs targeted the retired `:3000` listener — base URL now goes through pveproxy (`aaef676`); two gateway tests still encode direct-listener semantics, rewrite when next touched. **Review closure (2026-09-22, independent read-only review on the local lane, fixes in three batches `f27c3e0` / `e756b2b` / `0ab01d6`):** storage.cfg unreadable (pmxcfs down) now FAILS CLOSED — every dataset on every pool answers kind `config-unreadable`, mutations refuse, reads work (ENOENT stays open for non-PVE hosts); recursive verbs (destroy `-r`, recursive snapshot create, recursive schedules on create+update+run, backup snapshots) refuse when any DESCENDANT is owned — the nested-storage shape (`pool tank/x/data`) made the unowned parent a sweep vector; replication `snapshotFirst` on an owned source refused; `setPermissions`/`setAccess` guarded; SMB/NFS share create (and path-moving edit) refuse a path on an owned dataset (daemon backstop for the UI pickers); schedule and replication-task RUNS re-ask ownership (a storage registered after the schedule was created fails the run with the reason, never a silent skip); backup consistency on the one loader; `childrenManageable` stamped on every owned node from the same predicate (probe child) and pool-root ownership `pve` on Pool payloads. Suite 3637 daemon / 55 shared / 48 gateway; live proof re-run 12/12 on the final build, asserting `childrenManageable` per kind. **Second-pass review (2026-09-22, lane-swapped: the fix author never reviews; fixes `5835a33` + `c2fe2d9`):** no fail-safe regression, no over-refusal on ANAS-only hosts; closed: replication `snapshotFirst` re-asks ownership IN the job right before the snapshot (pre-job 400 kept as the zero-cost fast path, one footprint load per request); a failed descendant probe (`zfs list -r` non-zero) REFUSES the recursive verb instead of reading as "no descendants"; the system-pool sentence names the real cause when it fires from unreadable config; `legacy`/`none`-mountpoint datasets stay in the mountpoint table (resolved via findmnt) so a PVE dir storage on one is owned, and an unresolvable ZFS-backed dir storage is claimed by its configured path — while a dir storage on a NON-ZFS path (`/var/lib/vz` on ext4) stays shareable per the §2 caveat-emptor ruling, decided by the longest-prefix findmnt mount's fstype (unreadable table ⇒ tighten). Suite 3676 daemon; live proof 12/12 and UI acceptance 7/7 on the final build. **Result (2026-09-24/25; unit slice 3f78130 GLM + review fix batch 3e50042 opus + live proof ad6da91 opus):** `disk-identity-cache.ts` readings carry `measuredAt`; `isDue` fires past the cadence (30 min default, `ANAS_SMART_REPROBE_MS` clamped [1 s, 24 h] — `30m` no longer parses to a 30 ms storm); the re-probe is the same `-n standby` call, at most one per disk per pass, on every consumer that reports health (`loadMany`); `parsers/smartctl.ts` reads `power_mode.name` (the real smartctl 7.5 shape `{ata_value, name}` — the design text had said `.string` and the first fixture matched the fiction; corrected, fixture documented as documented-shape-not-capture); detail rows on both SMART shapes, none on standby; one `/health` source stashes `ANAS.daemonVersion` for the dashboard's "ANAS <version>" label. Live proof `disks-reprobe.spec.ts` 5/5 ×2 on the stunt node (a third run on the bare node 5/5): with a drop-in `ANAS_SMART_REPROBE_MS=2000`, two loads 3 s apart = two `smartctl` invocations per disk, 1 s apart = one, and without the drop-in the shipped 30-minute cadence = one; the count is taken by a test-side `smartctl` shim logging its argv under its own journald tag (the daemon logs no argv; `-u anasd` views drop short-lived children's lines, the tag holds them all); a hit returns the SAME `smartMeasuredAt`; the detail dates the reading (`SMART read 20s ago`) and shows no power-mode row on QEMU disks; the presence case is harness-proven on both detail shapes; the dashboard label equals `/v1/health`'s version; every daemon restart in the spec asserts the gateway came back (`PartOf=`). Ground truth corrected: QEMU disks are NOT a smartctl failure — `smartctl -n standby -iH --json` exits 0 with a SCSI identity (`smart_support.available: false`), so the measured path and the cadence age check governed, which is the stronger proof. No product defect.

**pvepool.2** `[done 2026-09-22]` Footprint ownership — Datasets workflow (design DESIGN.md "PVE footprint ownership" → Workflow) — as a user, I want the Datasets screen to show exactly what PVE owns and let me work everything else. **Build:** `60-datasets.js` stamps per-node ownership from the daemon's `pve` field via the shared predicate (fail-safe skew: pool `pveStorages[]` non-empty + no per-node field ⇒ today's whole-pool rule); badges "PVE storage root: <id>" / "PVE guest volume (<id>)" / "PVE storage (<id>)" with tooltips naming the storage; toolbar per node (root: Create enabled, rest view-only; guest/dir: Detail only; sibling: everything); Create-dataset pool picker includes PVE pools with the INFORMATIONAL note (I/O shared with guests; PVE's free-space figure for <id> shrinks by ANAS's usage); live field validation of the guest pattern with the "PVE would inventory `<name>` as a guest disk" sentence; the six UI `isPveManaged` copies (30-pools, 60-datasets, 65-replication, 69-snapshots, 67-mounts, inline 75-iscsi) replaced by the one predicate — Replication / Snapshot-schedule / add-LUN pickers list siblings and omit owned datasets; Pools-grid badge tooltip gains "datasets outside PVE's footprint are manageable in Datasets"; tree reloads after every job, success AND failure. **Acceptance:** Playwright spec on the pvepool.1 stunt-node fixture: badges per kind, toolbar enable/disable per node kind, create-with-note happy path ending with the new node selected, naming-guard field message before submit, reload after an injected job failure (stand-in `zfs` failing create); skew spec with the per-node field stripped from the payload ⇒ whole-pool gating. Dialog-contract harness updated for the new note and validator. **Result:** `ANAS.pve` in `10-api.js` = the one client-side helper (`ownership` / `isOwned` / `badge` / `wouldBeClaimed`; skew signal = "any row in the payload carries `pve`", and a pool ROW always takes the whole-pool rule — pool-level actions stay disabled on PVE pools); six UI copies deleted. `60-datasets.js`: per-node ownership, badges per kind, per-kind toolbar (Create enabled on the storage root), create dialog lists PVE pools with the informational note and live naming guard, tree reloads on success AND failure with the new node selected. `65-replication` / `69-snapshots` / `75-iscsi` pickers list siblings and omit owned datasets (a guest-NAMED zvol on a non-PVE pool is a valid backing — ownership, not names, decides, matching the daemon). Harnesses: dialog-contracts 1350, edit-retarget 98, pve-ownership 60. Acceptance `tests/integration/pvepool-ui.spec.ts` 7/7 on the stunt node (badges, toolbar per kind, create happy path with selection, naming guard, reload after a failed job, skew ⇒ whole-pool gating incl. Create disabled on the root, pickers). Playwright notes recorded in the spec: toolbar overflow below ~1300 px hides Destroy/snapshot verbs from the DOM, so the spec runs at 2560×1080. Human pass by the operator pending. **Review closure (2026-09-22, opus read-only review, fix `d5619c6`):** Create is keyed on the daemon's `childrenManageable` for the target PARENT in both the toolbar and the dialog (was keyed on the selected row's kind — a system pool root wrongly refused `rpool/media`, and switching the pool combo inside the dialog slipped past the toolbar gate); kind `config-unreadable` has its own label (was rendered as "boot filesystem"); the Pools grid reads pool-root `pve` (system / unreadable / dir-on-root pools were unbadged with Export/Destroy enabled until the 409); reload preserves expansion + selection and expands the new node's ancestors; 65/69 dataset-list failure path returns nothing for a PVE pool; the restore door's new-LUN pool picker applies ownership; owned-row tooltips carry the reason; no dead expander on owned rows; `ANAS.pve.storagesOf` replaces the last two private copies. Harnesses 1350 / 98 / 79; UI acceptance re-run 7/7 on the final build. **Second-pass review (lane-swapped, fix `5747834`):** nothing of substance; guarded skew read, picker catch branches tighten, and the harness now pins mid-skew Create, the dialog parent gate and expansion restore (dialog-contracts 1376).

**topology.1** `[done 2026-09-25]` Topology zoo (0.3.5 post-mortem ruling (b); test infrastructure, slips freely) — consolidate the pool-shape fixtures already captured (`zpool-status-all-vdev-classes`, `mirrored-log-partitions`, `multi-cache-spare`, `spare-active`, the older data-only/mirror/raidz/degraded set, the iostat pairs) with the shapes still missing (file vdevs, a by-id whole-disk pool, a suspended pool's `zpool status -j` and `zpool iostat -plv` — the one capture that settles the all-dash header question) into one documented zoo under `fixtures/zfs/` with a `NOTES.md` provenance table and matching `vdev-fixture.sh` verbs; a parser test iterates the zoo asserting a stable output shape per fixture, and every consumer test that reasons about topology draws from it. **Acceptance:** the zoo table in `NOTES.md`; one parser test over all entries; a stunt-node capture script that re-captures the zoo on demand (`test/stunt-node/topology-zoo.sh capture`) so a ZFS upgrade re-proves the shapes. **Result (2026-09-24/25; slice 1 c4dce51 + review fix batch 4bca31d + capture run b2c28fe):** `fixtures/zfs/NOTES.md` is the zoo: a provenance row per fixture (captured vs synthetic vs canonical, ZFS version, story, what it pins — the old blanket "all synthetic" claim was wrong), a "Capture argv" section (the daemon issues `zpool status -jv`; the three 2.4.4 captures from 0.3.5 were taken with `-j -p` and stay beside their `-jv` re-captures because the parser output differs: `-p` gives an int `pool_guid` and byte sizes, `-jv` a string guid and display sizes — the flag, not the version), and Missing shapes = none. `zpool-status-zoo.test.ts` is directory-driven both ways (a fixture without a table entry or without a NOTES row fails by name; 167 cases incl. the iostat pairs parsed without `knownPools`). Six new captures from the stunt node under `-jv`: all-vdev-classes, multi-cache-spare, mirrored-log-partitions, file-vdev, byid-whole-disk, suspended, each with its `zpool iostat -plv` pair. Settled: a SUSPENDED pool's `zpool status -jv` returns in 9 ms with `errors: List of errors unavailable` on stderr, `errlist` = the errno string, root `CANT_OPEN`/`NO_REPLICAS` over a `FAULTED` leaf; its iostat pool row carries REAL counters, so the all-dash-header question is answered no — a suspended pool cannot be misread as a section header; ZFS accepts a file as log/cache/spare; an in-tree file vdev parses with zero leaves; a by-id whole-disk leaf is NAMED by the disk while `path`/`devid` say `-part1`. The parser handled every real capture unmodified. Capture tooling proven on its first run and fixed where it broke: `zpool scrub` on a suspending pool blocks in uninterruptible D state, so `timeout` cannot kill it — the forcing scrub is launched detached and the state poll is the completion test; the dm-error mechanism and the documented recovery (restore the linear table → clear → destroy) took about one second from a wedged shape; backticks in an unquoted `bash -s` heredoc comment executed as command substitutions ON THE DESKTOP (removed, header warning added); `add-disk.sh` reused an undersized leftover image silently (now grown); `start_anasd` starts the gateway too (`PartOf=` propagates stop, never start). Daemon 4606.

**ahrexpand.1** `[done 2026-09-25]` Zero-gain expand guard (candidate since 2026-08, ruled 2026-09-21) — an AHR expansion plan whose reachable target adds no usable capacity (the §5.2 "pending capacity" shape: one disk replaced above a band boundary, or a disk set the planner cannot use) is refused up front with the guiding 409 that states the exact shortfall and what unlocks it — the planner's own pending-capacity line, lifted verbatim ("2 TiB of new capacity is pending — replace one more disk with ≥4 TiB to unlock ~2 TiB"; on AHR-2 the same shape owes three disks and says so) — instead of a sub-second "success" that did nothing. The unlock is modelled the way the plan is shaped: a replace is unlocked by another replace (band members are swapped, not added, so only bands above the top boundary form), an addition by another addition. The gain is measured planner-vs-planner and a plan with no steps has no gain, so growth an abandoned expansion left owed is never credited to a new plan. A confirm-code bypass is allowed for the honest zero-gain cases an operator may still want (a replace for a failing disk of the same size); the guided replace is a repair verb and keeps its own headline, carrying the zero-gain fact as a warning. UI-facing safety gate on a dangerous op, so it keeps full weight (§2 API-for-UI rule). Plan-time only; the executor is untouched. **Result (live proof 2026-09-25, stunt node):** `tests/integration/ahr-expand-zero-gain.spec.ts` 3/3 twice over `test/stunt-node/ahrexpand-fixture.sh` (AHR-1 `gtexpand` on two 2 GiB disks, a 4 GiB disk to replace one with — the granularity is 1 GiB, so the sizes round exactly and every figure is the planner's). The §5.2 shape: `POST /expand/plan` with `replace` answers `usableGain: 0` and a `zeroGain` block whose `shortfall` is `This plan adds no usable capacity: ` + the planner's own warning *"2 GiB of new capacity is pending — replace one more disk with ≥4 GiB to unlock ~2 GiB"*, lifted verbatim (asserted string-equal against `plan.warnings`), `unlockSize` 4 GiB, `unlockGain` 2 GiB, the unlock said as a REPLACE because the plan is one; the resulting top band is [2 GiB, 4 GiB] with one member and no level. `POST /expand` is a 409 `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED` whose MESSAGE is that shortfall and whose warnings carry the pending sentence; resent with `X-Anas-Confirm-Code` it is a 202 whose job completes — the disk set really swapped (4 GiB in, 2 GiB out), raw capacity grew 4→6 GiB, usable stayed 1.99 GiB and 2 GiB is reported pending, and no intent file is left. Adding the freed 2 GiB disk back is the positive counterpart: `usableGain` 2 GiB exactly (raid1×2 → raid5×3), no `zeroGain`, and the 409 it still gets (expansion is a Principle-14 dangerous op — the gate is unchanged, only its message) reads *"Expanding AHR pool 'gtexpand' starts an online reshape"*. The run also demonstrated WHY the guard is planner-vs-planner: on the zero-gain plan the naive `after.usableBytes - before.usableBytes` reads **+8 MiB** (band math against the live volume's LVM overhead) while the verdict is 0 — the subtraction the wizard would otherwise show. Playwright at 2560×1080: the wizard's Change row reads *no usable capacity* with the shortfall sentence beneath it, and the Execute confirm dialog carries the pending sentence verbatim. **One gap, not fixed:** the confirm dialog renders the 409's `warnings`, never its `message`, so the *"This plan adds no usable capacity: …"* headline itself appears only in the wizard's plan preview; the pending sentence it is built from does reach the dialog (twice — once through `warnGate.applyToConfirm`, once in `expansionWarnings`). Closing it would mean `confirmAndRun` rendering `error.message` for every flow, which is wider than this story.

**identity.3** `[done 2026-09-26]` Passdb entry must match the account exactly (GitHub #68, reproduced 2026-09-25 on the stunt node; follow-on to identity.1–2) — as a user who recreates a share user under a name that differs only in case from an earlier one, I want ANAS to notice that Samba's password database still holds the old entry, so a capitalized user never turns into a guest session. **Ground truth:** tdbsam keys entries by the lowercased name but stores the Unix username as first written; `smbpasswd -a <Name>` on an orphan entry stored as `<name>` prints `Failed to find a Unix account for <name>` and still EXITS 0, leaving the stored name unchanged and the uid unmapped (`pdbedit -L` shows `4294967295`); at logon Samba accepts the password, `getpwnam(<name>)` fails (`NT_STATUS_NO_SUCH_USER`), Debian's default `map to guest = bad user` makes the session a guest, Windows refuses guest sessions (network error), a Linux listing still succeeds as guest; a lowercase user can never reach the state. Today ANAS: create checks only `getent`, set-password reports success, the case-folded `passdbHas` (identity.1) shows the password as set, and user delete (`smbpasswd -x`) FAILS on such an entry while `pdbedit -x -u` succeeds. **Build (daemon, `routes/share-identity.ts` + `parsers/pdbedit.ts`):** (1) after every `smbpasswd -a`/set, read `pdbedit -L -u <name>` and require the first field to equal the name EXACTLY and the uid to equal `getent`'s; on a mismatch run `pdbedit -x -u <name>` and `smbpasswd -a` again, then re-verify, failing the job with a sentence naming the stored name if it still differs; (2) create refuses up front when passdb holds an entry for the name in any case whose stored name differs (`pdbedit -L` is already read by `passdbHas` — add an exact-case variant beside it, one parser home), with the sentence naming the stored entry and the fix, unless the create can repair it under (1) — pick the repair, it is what the operator would do by hand; (3) user delete drops the SMB entry with `pdbedit -x -u` (exact) instead of `smbpasswd -x`, so an orphan or mismatched entry never blocks the delete (identity.2 order kept: passdb first, then `userdel`); (4) `smbEnabled` on `ShareUser` reads true only for an exact-case match with a mapped uid — a mismatched entry reports `smbEnabled: false` with a `smbEntryMismatch: '<stored name>'` field (additive) so the Share Users grid can say "SMB entry stored as '<name>' — set the password again to repair" instead of a green tick. **Workflow (§2 rule):** *Where:* Share Users screen, no new screen. *Happy path:* create or set password → the job repairs a stale entry silently and reports success only after the exact-case check passes. *Failure as the user meets it:* the job fails with the stored name and the one-line fix; the grid row shows the mismatch text instead of the tick. *Timeliness:* the grid reads it on every load (pull). **Acceptance:** unit tests over `pdbedit -L` fixtures (exact match / case-mismatched orphan / unmapped uid / absent), the create and set-password jobs' argv sequences (repair path = `pdbedit -x` then `smbpasswd -a` then the verify read; clean path unchanged and pinned), delete's new argv, `smbEnabled`/`smbEntryMismatch` on the list; **live proof on the stunt node** (`identity-passdb.spec.ts`): stage the orphan by hand exactly as reproduced (`useradd name` → `smbpasswd -a name` → `userdel name`), create `Name` through the API → `pdbedit -L` shows `Name:<uid>`, `smbclient //node/share -U Name` lists and writes (no guest), Windows-shaped `-U 'WINPC\Name' -m SMB3` too; a mismatched entry planted after create → grid shows the mismatch, set-password repairs it; delete on a mismatched entry succeeds. Cross-check (§2): TrueNAS creates its Samba users through its own middleware and never exposes this state; the closest operator habit is `pdbedit -x` + re-add, which the repair replicates. Reply to #68 posted 2026-09-25 asking the reporter to confirm the stored name; the issue stays open until this ships. Release vehicle = operator's call (0.4.0 if the reporter confirms before the cut, else a point release per the §2 shipped-feature rule). Lane: opus (the passdb verify is judgment); live proof local. **Result (2026-09-26):** built, unit-proven, live proof `identity-passdb.spec.ts` 5/5 ×2 on the stunt node 2026-09-26 (orphan staged by hand, create repairs it with one journal line, smbclient lists and writes as the user and as `WINPC\\Name` over SMB3, a planted mismatch reads `smbEntryMismatch` in the API and the grid and refuses the login, set-password repairs it, delete over a mismatch leaves no entry and no account; real `pdbedit -L` lines match the fixture shape) (`tests/integration/identity-passdb.spec.ts`, written, not yet run on the stunt node). The daemon now reads `pdbedit -L` once through one parser (`parsePdbeditEntries` → `passdbEntry` found case-folded, `passdbServes` = exact stored name AND the account's uid, `passdbOwnedByOther` = mapped to a different uid; `parsePdbeditNames`/`passdbHas` retired into them). Create and set-password run `smbpasswd -a -s` then `getent passwd <name>` + `pdbedit -L`; a mismatch runs `pdbedit -x -u <stored>`, `smbpasswd -a -s` again and re-verifies, logging one journald line naming the replaced entry, and the job result gains `smbEntryReplaced`; a repair that still does not verify fails the job naming the stored entry and the `pdbedit -x -u` fix. Create without an SMB password over a case-mismatched orphan is a 409 (`passdb-orphan`) naming the entry and both fixes. Found along the way and closed in the same change: an entry Samba resolves to ANOTHER live account's uid (accounts `pat` and `Pat`) is that account's password — `smbpasswd -a Pat` would have changed `pat`'s; create and set-password now refuse it up front (409 `passdb-entry-owned`), and delete and enable/disable leave it alone. Delete drops the entry by its stored name with `pdbedit -x -u` (identity.2 order kept, absent skipped). `ShareUser.smbEnabled` is true only for an exact mapped match; an orphan or case-mismatched entry adds `smbEntryMismatch: '<stored>'` (additive, shared schema), and the Share Users grid shows "SMB entry stored as '<name>' — set the password again to repair" in place of the tick (harness-checked). The identity.1b reading (a case-different entry counts as SMB-enabled) is reversed by this story, and its unit tests with it.

**rclone.4** `[done 2026-09-26]` Guided remote form (operator ask during the 2026-09-25 Google Drive human pass; follows rclone.1) — as a user adding a cloud remote, I want the dialog to show the few fields my backend actually needs, with one sentence telling me where each value comes from, so I am not staring at client IDs and service-account files when all Drive wants is a token. **Today:** the dialog renders rclone's own option list for the chosen backend and hides what rclone flags `advanced`; that is adaptive at rclone's level, but rclone's "basic" set for an OAuth backend still lists `client_id`, `client_secret`, `scope` and `service_account_file` beside `token`, which only matter to someone bringing their own OAuth client. **Cross-check (§2 rule):** TrueNAS's cloud-credentials form curates a per-provider field set (Drive: client id/secret optional + access token behind a "log in to provider" button; S3: access key, secret key, endpoint; SFTP: host, port, user, password/key) — same shape; the browser login button is not ours to copy (the daemon is headless, the paste flow stays). **Mechanism:** ONE curation table, `packages/shared/src/cloud-guide.ts`, `{ [backend]: { guide: string, essential: string[], ownClient?: string[] } }`, consumed by the daemon's catalogue trim so the UI stays a renderer: `CloudProvider.guide?: string` and `CloudProviderOption.essential: boolean` (additive; uncurated backend ⇒ `essential = !advanced`, no guide — today's behaviour byte-identical for every backend not in the table). First curated set: `drive`, `onedrive`, `dropbox`, `box`, `pcloud` (essential: `token`; ownClient: `client_id`, `client_secret`), `s3` (`provider`, `access_key_id`, `secret_access_key`, `region`, `endpoint` — the sub-provider examples already ride the options), `b2` (`account`, `key`), `sftp` (`host`, `port`, `user`, `pass`, `key_file`), `ftp` (`host`, `port`, `user`, `pass`), `webdav` (`url`, `vendor`, `user`, `pass`), `smb` (`host`, `user`, `pass`, `domain`), `azureblob` (`account`, `key`); each with a one-sentence `guide` naming where the values come from (Drive: the `rclone authorize` paste, per rclone.1's fix; S3: the provider's access-key page; SFTP: the server login). **Workflow (§2 rule):** *Where:* the Add/Edit remote dialog, no new screen. *Happy path:* pick `drive` → the guide sentence, the token field (rclone.1's paste rule), Test, Save; the own-client fields sit under a collapsed "Use your own OAuth client" row with one sentence (optional; rclone's built-in client is shared by every rclone user and rate-limited, a client of your own avoids that); every other non-advanced option under a collapsed "More options"; "Show advanced options" unchanged. Required fields keep their mark; an uncurated backend renders exactly as today. *Edit:* same layout, secrets "(unchanged)" as today. *Failure:* unchanged (Test verdict, 400 sentences). **Acceptance:** trim tests (curated ⇒ `essential` + `guide`; uncurated ⇒ `essential` mirrors `!advanced`, no `guide`; a curated name absent from rclone's list is ignored, never invented); harness contracts (drive: token primary, client fields in the own-client group, service-account under More options; s3: the five primary fields; an uncurated backend: today's layout); Playwright on the stunt node over the drive and s3 forms (extend `cloud-remotes-ui.spec.ts` or its sibling). Lane: design is settled above; GLM for the slice (shared table + trim + UI + harness), local for the Playwright run; opus review. **Result (2026-09-25):** the table is `packages/shared/src/cloud-guide.ts` (`CLOUD_BACKEND_GUIDES`, 12 backends — the five OAuth ones essential `token`, with the `client_id`/`client_secret` pair as `ownClient` on drive/onedrive/pcloud; s3 five essential fields; b2 and azureblob two; sftp five; ftp, webdav and smb four each), exported from the shared index with a table test (lowercase snake_case, no name twice, one-sentence guides); `CloudProviderOption` gained additive optional `essential`, `CloudProvider` additive optional `guide`/`ownClient` (the own-client list rides the provider so the dialog stays a pure renderer — no second table in the UI), and the daemon's `trimProviders` sets them: curated ⇒ the table's list + the sentence, uncurated ⇒ `essential = !advanced` and nothing added, a curated name absent from rclone's list never looked up; the dialog renders guide sentence → essential fields → collapsed "Use your own OAuth client" fieldset (with its one sentence) → collapsed "More options" fieldset → the advanced toggle unchanged, provider sub-filters working inside every group and an uncurated backend byte-identical (the token exception and the paste normalisation untouched; a collapsed fieldset still holds its fields, so a typed own-client value rides the create body with the group shut). Verification green: shared 116, daemon 4664 (six new curation tests over the real 1.60.1 capture), dialog-contracts 1912 (+20), typecheck + eslint clean; the Playwright proof on the node is the local lane's run. **Live proof (2026-09-26, a9b9904):** `cloud-guided-viewer.spec.ts` tests 1–3 on the node: drive (one instruction sentence, token alone in the primary section, own-client fieldset collapsed with its sentence, scope and service-account under More options, root_folder_id behind Advanced), s3 (the five primary fields in order, env_auth under More options), koofr uncurated (pre-story layout byte-for-byte). **Rider (2026-09-26, human-pass finding):** a remote saved with a made-up `client_id` (the word `gdrive`) silently overrides rclone's built-in client, and a refresh token issued by the built-in one can never be refreshed under it — Google answers every refresh an hour later with `invalid_client`, and the Test verdict cannot catch it (it does not refresh) — so the OAuth five's own-client fields are validated AT SAVE TIME, at both boundaries: ONE shared rule, `validateOwnClient` in `cloud-guide.ts` (`client_id` empty or the provider's own shape — Google `…apps.googleusercontent.com`, Microsoft a UUID, Dropbox/Box/pCloud any whitespace-free token — and `client_id`/`client_secret` set together, a lone either refused), run by the daemon's create/update/test doors as a 400 with the sentence before anything is written or probed, and by the dialog's ES5 port on blur and before Test/Save, both pinned to the shared `test-vectors/own-client.json`.

**rclone.5** `[done 2026-09-26]` Cancel a running run (operator ask during the 2026-09-25 human pass; follows the rclone.3 Run-now gate) — as a user watching a cloud sync run I did not mean to start, or one that will take hours, I want to stop it from the same toolbar, behind a confirmation that shows me what the stop throws away, so an accidental click cannot cost me a fourteen-hour upload. **Ground truth:** the run's rclone process is a child of anasd (the direct job runs it under the executor; the unit only supervises), so `systemctl stop` on the unit kills the runner and NOT rclone — cancellation has to reach the job. The job queue has no cancel today. **Cross-check (§2 rule):** TrueNAS cloud sync rows carry Run Now, Stop and Logs; Stop asks for confirmation and marks the run aborted. **Mechanism:** the job queue gains cancellation: a job may register an `onCancel` hook when it starts; `POST /v1/jobs/:id/cancel` (new, additive; DESIGN §API gains the row) is **confirm-gated like every irreversible verb** — the first call answers 409 with `X-Anas-Confirm-Code` and a headline naming the task, how long the run has been going and how far it is ("cloud sync task 'photos' has been running 2 h 14 m — 1,203 of 9,800 files, 3.1 GB of 12.4 GB copied; cancelling stops it here"); the replay with the code runs the hook and the job ends with status `cancelled` (additive enum value on the shared Job schema; the dashboard strip renders it). A job without a hook answers 409 `not-cancellable` with the sentence; a finished job answers 409 with its final state. The cloud direct run job registers a hook that sends SIGINT to the rclone child (rclone stops after its in-flight transfers; a second SIGINT after 10 s if it has not exited; the transient snapshot is destroyed in the existing `finally`), and the run ends `cancelled` with "cancelled by <user> at <time>"; the runner sees the job's final state and exits with code 130, and the status derivation maps that exit to `TaskRunResult` `cancelled` (additive) so the row reads cancelled, never failed. No notification on a cancel — the operator did it and the row says so (TrueNAS matches). **Backup parity (same story, second slice):** the backup direct run registers the same hook (SIGINT to `proxmox-backup-client`; PBS discards the unfinished snapshot), the same exit-code mapping, the same verb on the Backup toolbar. **Workflow (§2 rule):** *Where:* the Tasks toolbar gains **Cancel run**, enabled only on a running row (Run now is disabled on that row, rclone.3); the run viewer (rclone.6) carries the same button. *Confirm:* the standard `confirmAndRun` dialog over the 409 — the headline above plus the consequence sentence: "Files already copied stay at the destination. A sync run stopped part-way leaves the destination between two states until the next run completes." — a plain Yes/No is NOT enough here (operator ruling 2026-09-25: a mis-click on a long run must be caught by the dialog, and the dialog must show what is being thrown away). *Happy path:* Cancel run → the dialog with the run's elapsed time and progress → confirm → the job ends within seconds, the row reads cancelled with the time and user in the tooltip, the grid reloads. *Failure:* the 409 sentences in the toast (nothing running any more; not cancellable); a hook that fails to stop the child within the second signal fails the cancel with the reason and the run continues. **Acceptance:** queue tests (409 + code on the first call, hook called once on the replay, cancelled state; 409 for no hook / finished); direct-run tests (SIGINT to the child, second signal on a timeout, snapshot `finally` still runs, `cancelled` result); runner exit code and derivation tests; harness (button gating; the dialog carries elapsed + progress + the consequence sentence; the static guard that every `confirmAndRun` verb targets a confirm-gated route stays green); **live proof on the stunt node:** a `--bwlimit 200k` task against the loopback sftp remote → Cancel run → the dialog names the elapsed time and file count → confirm → rclone gone within 2 s, the unit's result and the row read cancelled, the partial files present at the destination, no notification in the journal; the same on a backup task with a `pbsgt` repository. **Result (2026-09-25):** built in one commit, live proof on the stunt node pending. **Queue:** a job body registers ONE cancel hook through the context the queue now passes beside `updateProgress` (`ctx.onCancel(hook, { subject, consequence })`, `ctx.cancellation()`); `JobQueue.cancel(id, user)` runs it once, and when it resolves the job ends `cancelled` whatever the body does next (its `finally` still runs) with `result = { status: 'cancelled', reason: 'cancelled by <user> at <time>' }`, `error` null, audit `job.cancelled` naming the canceller and the submitter; a hook that throws withdraws the cancel and the job runs on; `JobCancelledError` lets a body report that the work it WATCHED was cancelled (the Run-Now supervisor seeing the runner's 130). `cancelled` is additive on the shared `JobStatus`. **Route:** `POST /v1/jobs/:id/cancel` (routes/jobs.ts) — 404 unknown, `409 NOT_CANCELLABLE` / `409 CONFLICT` (finished, with its final state) before any code is minted, then the standard `confirmGate` 409 whose message is the headline "<subject> has been running for <elapsed> — <progress>; cancelling stops it here" and whose `warnings` are the headline plus the job's consequence sentence; the replay submits a `job.cancel` CONTROL job (runs at once, outside the concurrency limit — a Run-Now holds two slots, so a queued cancel could wait behind the runs it is meant to stop) that fails with the hook's reason when the run could not be stopped. **The hook — ONE helper, `packages/daemon/src/jobs/child-cancel.ts`** (`ChildCancel` + `stopChild`): SIGINT, wait 10 s, SIGINT again, wait 10 s, then fail "… did not stop after two SIGINTs 10 s apart — the run continues" (never SIGKILL); a child already gone fails the cancel ("the run is finishing on its own"); a cancel accepted before the child spawns stops it the moment it appears. The executor gained an optional `ExecOptions.onSpawn` handing back a `SpawnedChild` (pid, `kill`, `exited()`, `exit` promise) — prod on both exec paths, the mock gained `live` fixtures that record signals. Cloud's direct run passes it to the rclone exec, backup's to the `prlimit -- proxmox-backup-client` exec (prlimit execs in place, so the pid is pbc's); a cancelled run skips the notification (and backup's prune) and logs one journald line. **Exit-code mapping:** the runners (`dist/cloud-task.js`, `dist/backup-task.js`) treat `cancelled` as terminal, print the result line and exit **130** (`TASK_CANCELLED_EXIT_CODE` in `@anas/shared`); units are NOT re-rendered (130 is not declared `SuccessExitStatus=`, so systemd records `Result=exit-code`) and `deriveTaskRunResult` maps `ExecMainStatus=130` to the additive run result `cancelled` (also on `Result=success`), never `failure` — no dashboard warning; the status carries `lastRunNote` ("cancelled by <user> at <time>", read from the runner's journal line, only for a cancelled row) and `runningJobId` (the in-flight direct job's id, from the same lookup that fills `runningProgress`), both additive on `CloudSyncTaskView` / `BackupTaskEntry` / `BackupTaskDetail`. **Backup parity:** same hook, same exit code, same derivation, same no-notification rule, same toolbar verb. **UI:** one shared door `ANAS.sched.cancelRun` in `69-schedules-common.js` used by both grids — **Cancel run** on the Cloud Sync and Backup toolbars, enabled only on a running row whose `runningJobId` is known; the standard `confirmAndRun` WINDOW (not a plain Yes/No: "Keep running" is the default button, "Cancel run" the other — `confirmWindow` gained an additive `cancelButtonText`) showing the daemon's headline and consequence; the grid reloads on completion and again after 12 s (the runner's 10 s poll), and on failure; the Last run cell renders a neutral `cancelled` pill with `lastRunNote` (or the time) as the tooltip; `pollJob` handles `cancelled` (its own `onCancelled`, used by both Run-now handlers to reload, else a toast); the dashboard strip renders `cancelled` in a neutral class (was the failure red). The static confirm-door guard now also parses the shared door and asserts `POST /jobs/:id/cancel` is confirm-gated. **Tests:** queue (hook once, cancelled state + audit, 409 no-hook / finished / in-progress, hook failure withdraws, control job), child-cancel (ladder over mock live children and one real `sleep` via the prod executor), route (409 + code + headline + consequence, replay cancels, single-use code, failed hook), cloud + backup route end-to-end (grid names `runningJobId`, SIGINT on the replay, `cancelled`, no notification, no prune), runner-level (transient snapshot still destroyed on a cancel, cloud and backup), derivation (130 ⇒ cancelled on either Result, `lastRunNote`, supervisor ⇒ `JobCancelledError`), runner exit (poll stops at cancelled, `main()` exits 130 with the result line), harness (button gating on running/idle/cancelled rows, the real `confirmAndRun` window carries headline + consequence with "Keep running" default, the resend carries the code, reloads, the cancelled pill in both grids, the strip). **Closure (2026-09-26):** live proofs `cloud-cancel.spec.ts` 4/4 ×2 (2ef90c7: 409 code + consequence, replay, SIGINT within 5 s, unit exit 130, row cancelled with note, partial destination, no notification; refusals; dialog with Keep running as default; plus the runner-poll interruption sentence after a daemon restart) and `backup-cancel.spec.ts` 2/2 ×2 (3853a28: the same ladder against `pbsgt` with `proxmox-backup-client` — no new PBS snapshot, no prune, no notification, no transient snapshot left; PBS ingests ~225 MiB/s so the proof writes 3 GiB of fresh random data per run and cancels as soon as the client is visible). Independent review (lane-swapped) found no data-losing or failure-mis-flagging defect; polish batch 431e7e1: an accepted cancel is honoured at every pre-flight boundary (no snapshot, no exec), a spawn failure after an accepted cancel reads failed with the cancel noted, an unsignallable child fails the cancel instead of claiming it, a queued job's headline never says running. Known: a cancelled unit records exit 130 as `Result=exit-code` in systemd until its next run (declaring 130 a success would rewrite every unit); the row reads cancelled regardless. **0.4.1:** that leftover is settled at the source — after a cancel the daemon waits (bounded, 40 s) for the unit to go terminal and `reset-failed`s it when the exit was 130, so the unit leaves `systemctl --failed` while the row keeps reading cancelled (`clearCancelledRunFailedState` in task-units.ts, called detached from each direct run body's cancelled branch; the exit code survives the reset, so the derivation is untouched).

**rclone.6** `[done 2026-09-26]` Run viewer (operator ask during the 2026-09-25 human pass; ruling the same day: a live display, not a log) — as a user with a run in progress, I want to open it and see at a glance how far along it is, how fast, how long it has run and how long is left, which files are moving right now and what just finished or failed, so a seventeen-hour Drive upload is something I can watch rather than a pill. **Ground truth (rclone 1.60, captured 2026-09-25):** every `--stats` interval's JSON object carries `bytes`, `totalBytes`, `transfers`, `totalTransfers`, `checks`, `totalChecks`, `deletes`, `errors`, `elapsedTime`, `speed`, `eta`, `fatalError`, `retryError` and `transferring[]` with `name`, `size`, `bytes`, `percentage`, `speed`, `eta` per in-flight file; with `-v` rclone also emits one JSON event per finished file (`msg: "Copied (new)"` / `"Updated"` / `"Deleted"`, `object: <path>`, level info) and one per error. rclone's stream goes to the daemon's pipe, not journald, so `-v` costs the daemon one parsed line per file and the journal nothing. **Mechanism:** the direct run job publishes a typed, additive `detail` on the Job (shared `CloudRunDetail`: the counters above, `startedAt`, `lastError?`, `transferring[]`, `recent[]` = a ring of the last 50 per-file events `{ name, kind: copied|updated|deleted|error, at, message? }`, and `speedSamples[]` = the last 60 `speed` readings, one per stats object, i.e. the last 5 minutes at `--stats 5s` — in-memory on the job like every other job field, never written anywhere), updated on every stats object and file event; `GET /v1/jobs/:id` returns it (no new endpoint); the run's argv gains `-v` and `--stats 5s` (was 30 s — the window must move; the exit-code policy and everything else unchanged; DESIGN's argv row updated). Nothing is retained after the job leaves the queue (stateless): a finished run's summary is the journal line the runner already writes and the existing recent-runs list in the detail window; the viewer says so. **Cross-check (§2 rule):** TrueNAS's Logs dialog shows rclone's raw log for the task; ours shows the same facts as a live display, which is the ANAS-shaped answer, and the raw lines stay reachable through `journalctl -u anas-cloud-<name>`. **Workflow (§2 rule):** *Where:* the Tasks toolbar gains **View run** (enabled on a running row; the running text on the row opens it too) → `anas-win-cloud-run`, laid out as a dashboard, top to bottom: (1) the header line — task, `source → remote:path`, mode, live/snapshot label, a status pill (running / completed / failed / cancelled); (2) the **overall progress bar** (bytes of total, with the percentage in the bar) and a thin second bar for files (done of total); (3) the **throughput spark** — the dashboard's own sparkline (the `pushSpark` buffer + renderer in `50-dashboard.js` move to `15-gfx.js` as the ONE spark component; the dashboard's rendering stays byte-identical) fed from `speedSamples`, with the current speed labeled beside it and a **"stalled for N s"** label in danger styling when the last 3 or more samples are zero while files remain (operator ask 2026-09-25: a stalled run must show quickly); (4) one row of **labeled figures** — speed, ETA, elapsed, files done of total, checks, deletes, errors (errors in danger styling when non-zero) — every number carries its label and unit (UI rule: numbers need labeled context; no unlabeled tiles); (5) **Transferring now** — one row per in-flight file with the file name, size, an inline progress bar with its percentage, and its speed and ETA (rclone's `--transfers` default is 4, so this is at most a handful of rows); (6) **Recent** — the last 10 finished or failed files (name, what happened, how long ago), errors listed first in danger styling with the message, a "show all 50" link; (7) the last error line when there is one; buttons **Cancel run** (rclone.5, the confirm-gated one) and Close. *Timeliness:* the window polls the job every 2 s while it runs (pull; no websocket) and every figure above moves with the poll; when the job ends the polling stops, the bars stand at their final values, the pill turns completed/failed/cancelled and a one-line result banner replaces the in-flight section (files and bytes transferred, duration, "N errors" or "no errors"). *Failure:* a run that fails shows rclone's error events in Recent and the last error in the banner; opening the viewer on a task whose job has already left the queue shows the journal summary (the recent-runs facts) under the sentence that per-file detail is only shown while a run is in progress. **The same spark in two more places (operator ask 2026-09-25):** the dashboard's jobs strip shows a small spark + current speed on every in-flight run whose job carries `speedSamples` (cloud today; backup when its client offers a rate), and the Cloud Sync task row shows a tiny one beside the running progress text from rclone.3 — both read the running job on the page's existing pull (the Cloud Sync grid polls its running rows' jobs every 5 s while a running row is on screen, the dashboard on its own cadence), and both show the stalled label the same way, so a stalled run is visible at a glance from any of the three. **Backup:** `proxmox-backup-client` reports per-archive percent and bytes, no per-file stream — the backup viewer is its own later slice with what the client gives (divergence where the tool differs, stated). **Acceptance:** parser tests over captured stats and file-event lines (the ring buffer, the counters, an error event, `--stats 5s` argv pinned); route test (`detail` on the job); harness (the window's bars, spark, figures and grids from a fixture detail at four moments — early, mid-run with an error, stalled (zero samples ⇒ the label), finished; the jobs-strip and task-row sparks from the same fixture; the dashboard's existing spark checks unchanged after the extraction; the polling stop on a finished job; the no-detail sentence; the running text on the row opens the window); **live proof on the stunt node:** a `--bwlimit 200k` task with ~20 files against the loopback sftp remote → View run shows the in-flight file's bar climbing, Recent filling as files complete, elapsed and ETA moving every poll, then the result banner; a run against a remote with a wrong password shows the error in the banner and in Recent. **Result (2026-09-25):** built in one commit — the runner parses every stats object and per-file event into a `CloudRunDetail` published on the job through the queue's `updateDetail` (rings: 50 recent events, 60 speed samples), the ONE spark component (`pushBuf`/`pushSpark`/`sparkFromSamples`/`stalledHtml`) lives in `15-gfx.js` with the dashboard's rendering byte-identical (telemetry harness green), the run argv gained `-v --stats 5s` (was 30 s); live proof on the stunt node pending. Review fix batch A (2026-09-25): speed samples are daemon-derived from byte deltas (rclone's `speed` is a decaying EWMA that never reads zero on a stall and freezes after a 60 s check-only gap), stalled = three zero deltas with files remaining, error lines without an object reach `lastError`/`recent`, 1.60's real Copied/Deleted messages mapped, fixtures captured on the node, detail published per stats tick. Review fix batch B (2026-09-25): Recent shows the newest events, a vanished job ends the viewer's poll, the prepare phase reads 'Preparing the run…', the row spark survives the quiet reload and stops with the view, one runningProgressHtml in the shared schedules module, the UI's token and stalled helpers run the shared test vectors, OAuth backends carry one instruction sentence. Ground truth from the proof (2026-09-26): a frozen rclone emits no stats at all, so the tracker now keeps its own 5 s clock and appends a zero sample for every silent interval — a hung process and a hung network both read stalled after 15 s; `lastStatsAt` rides the detail. **Live proof (2026-09-26, a9b9904):** `cloud-guided-viewer.spec.ts` 5/5 ×2 — viewer bars, spark, seven labeled figures, a transferring row whose percentage climbs, newest-first Recent, tiny spark on the row surviving the quiet reload, small spark on the dashboard strip, stalled label within 25 s of a SIGSTOP on rclone (and gone within 25 s of SIGCONT), result banner with polling stopped, and the no-detail sentence after a daemon restart. Proof-side ground truth: sftp truncates modtimes to whole seconds and rclone preserves the source modtime, so destination mtime order is not a completion-order witness.

### 0.4.2 — point release: two user reports on 0.4.1's Datasets work (bug on a shipped feature + a refinement of the release theme; operator 2026-10-05: "well and tightly scoped, refinement or fix for recent work")

Both arrived during the post-0.4.1 watch window (GitHub #70 and #71, 2026-09-28). Per §2 the bug-fix point release takes the next number and the decided slate shifts to 0.4.3. Same-shape as 0.3.4/0.3.5: fix at the source, live proof on the stunt node through the gateway, notes in product voice, bump by key. **Full-diff review (fresh Opus, operator order 2026-10-06, after per-story reviews had missed things):** 12 findings. Fixed before the tag: held-inside-policy no longer warns (planRetention, all scopes); a refused destroy on a swept or excluded dataset is a per-dataset note and a `warning` outcome, never a failed run; capped run output (names per dataset, notification list, one progress line per dataset); shared prune types; guard lists datasets only; journal rung bounded by the unit file's creation (a re-created id no longer inherits its predecessor's verdict); `disabled` precedence restored; one journal read per row and one result-line scanner; the Proxmox storage row never folds and small pools keep their order; the exclude hint states the cleanup; dashboard harness wired. **Test review (fresh Opus, operator order) → batch C (2acc846):** zone-independent systemd reads (every `systemctl show` under `TZ=UTC` in the child env only — `--timestamp=utc` proven inert for `show` on the fleet; the parser accepts UTC/GMT/Z/offset only; proven live on Europe/Berlin across a reboot), total caps on run output (200 pruned / 100 held names; per-dataset section ≤ 100 lines; residual: `datasets[]` itself is uncapped, ~500 noted datasets would exceed journald's line), already-gone destroys count as pruned, the fail-safe is a `warning`, a 0.4.1 UI PUT without `exclude` keeps the stored list (the 0.4.2 UI always sends it), `ANAS_REQUIRE_FIXTURES=1` in the proof runner turns fixture skips into failures (42 guards), two orphan harnesses + a last-run pill harness wired, provenance proven live (a received copy keeps the creation second; only the txg differs). **C8:** at 1366×768 the tree measured 19.7% (PVE's task log takes the bottom third) → responsive cap (max 3 under 600 px panel height) + a Hide chart toggle, remembered per browser. **C10(a):** a child's own hourly destroying the parent's dailies on that child was pinned as current behaviour and is retired by ident.1's absent-bucket rule. **Rulings:** swept children are pruned by name (the schedule took them with `-r`; the witness rule stays excluded-only, otherwise droppings could never converge) and the notes tell replica keepers to exclude the replica; the most-generous count is one-directional (the child's own run keeps its own policy) and the notes say so; no 409 on adding an exclude (hint instead); first-run convergence is long by nature and the notes say so, no batched destroys (comma-list destroy with a held member has unclear semantics).

**dshero.1** `[done 2026-10-05]` **(GitHub #70, bug)** As a user opening Datasets on a node whose pool also holds Proxmox guest volumes, I want the Pool space hero to keep a bounded height, so the dataset tree below it is usable. **Found** on a two-node cluster (0.4.1, screenshot in the issue): the hero legend emits one row per top-level dataset with no cap; on a PVE-storage pool that is every `subvol-*`/`vm-*`/`basevol-*` volume (≈60 rows), the hero — a plain component at natural height in the vbox — pushes the `flex: 1` tree to zero height and nothing scrolls. **Fix (two parts, both in `60-datasets.js`'s hero builder):** (1) fold every PVE-owned child (the tree node's `pveOwnership`, the same classification story 3.25's badge uses) into ONE segment labelled *Proxmox storage* (review: the verdict covers dir storage, storage roots and system datasets too, so the label is the neutral one) (its value = the sum of their `used`), so the breakdown reads the way the Name column already does — ANAS datasets by name, PVE's footprint as one block; (2) cap the legend to the largest N named segments (N = 6; was 8 until the 18-dataset live case showed the tree under half the panel) plus an *n more* roll-up row whose value is the remainder and whose tooltip lists the folded names, Free last; the donut draws the same folded segments so legend and ring agree. The hero height is then bounded regardless of dataset count, which is the actual invariant (a scrollable legend would hide the bug, not fix it). **Proof:** a Playwright spec against the pvepool fixture (`pvepool-fixture.sh`, the one with guest volumes) asserts the tree keeps ≥ half the panel height with a pool of many datasets, that one *Proxmox storage* row appears (never folded into the roll-up but it takes one of the N slots, since a 9-row legend measured the tree at 49.7%; small pools keep their pre-0.4.2 order — full review), and that the legend never exceeds N + 2 rows and the tree body keeps at least half the panel on a pool of 18 datasets; a unit test pins the fold/cap on a synthetic 60-dataset segment list. **Not in scope:** collapsing the hero, per-pool preferences, any change to the tree. **Proof:** harness + spec, green ×2 — the `gfx-cap-segments` unit harness (41 checks over the synthetic 60-segment list, incl. the donut/legend-agreement and free-last pins) wired into `test:unit:ui`, and `tests/integration/datasets-hero.spec.ts` against the pvepool fixture (one folded row, no guest-volume rows, ≤ 10 legend rows, tree ≥ half the panel height), green twice with `pvepool-ui.spec.ts` still green.

**snapx.1** `[done 2026-10-05]` **(GitHub #71, refinement)** As a user with one hourly recursive snapshot schedule on a pool, I want to exclude named child datasets (media, backup targets) from it, so I keep one schedule instead of one per dataset. **Today** a recursive schedule is a plain `zfs snapshot -r` (`zfs-snapshot.ts`, `snapshot-schedules.ts`), so there is no way to skip a child. **Mechanism:** add `exclude?: DatasetPath[]` to the schedule schema (shared, Zod; a schedule carries it in its `X-ANAS-Schedule=` JSON like every other field), validated at both boundaries: each entry must be a strict descendant of the target dataset, else 400 with the offending name. When `recursive` is true and `exclude` is non-empty, the runner expands the target's descendant list (`zfs list -H -o name -r -t filesystem,volume <target>`), drops every excluded dataset AND its descendants, and takes all the remaining snapshots in ONE `zfs snapshot name@snap name2@snap …` call — ZFS creates multiple snapshots named in one invocation atomically, so the schedule keeps the same consistency guarantee as `-r`. `recursive` without `exclude` stays the literal `-r` (unchanged behaviour, unchanged units for every existing schedule). **Guard interaction (the point of the feature):** the create/update guard today refuses a recursive schedule when any strict descendant is PVE-owned (pvepool.1 review fix 2); with an exclude list the guard runs on the descendant set MINUS the excluded subtrees, so "hourly on the pool, guest volumes excluded" is accepted and the refusal message, when it still fires, names the owned descendant and says it can be excluded. Prune today is `zfs destroy -r <target>@<name>`; verify live that a descendant lacking the snapshot (the excluded one) does not make the recursive destroy fail, else prune per dataset from the same expansion. Retention (`snapshot-retention.ts`) prunes by name, so excluded datasets simply never carry the schedule's snapshots; verify, don't assume. **UI:** on the schedule dialog, an *Exclude datasets* multi-select that appears when *Recursive* is ticked, listing the target's descendants from the tree the dialog already has (PVE-owned children shown with their badge, selectable — excluding guest volumes from an hourly schedule is exactly the use case); the grid's schedule row shows *recursive, N excluded* with the names in the tooltip. **Proof:** unit tests for the expansion (excluded subtree dropped, non-descendant rejected, empty exclude = `-r`); live on the stunt node through the gateway: a schedule with one excluded child fires, `zfs list -t snapshot` shows the snapshot on the target and the other children and NOT on the excluded one or its child; a Playwright spec drives the dialog. **Not in scope:** excludes on non-recursive schedules (400), AHR schedules (n/a, whole `@data`), replication or backup exclude lists. **Shipped as designed** with two review-driven refinements: on PUT a stored exclude entry whose dataset is gone is tolerated (a plain enable/disable toggle must not 400) while a NEW missing entry is refused; the picker lists PVE-owned children with their badge because excluding them is the use case. Proof: 9 route tests + expansion/units/notify tests; `schedules-exclude.spec.ts` (dialog) green ×2; `schedules-exclude-api.spec.ts` (guard refusal naming the nested storage root, acceptance with it excluded, one fire snapshots target + kept sibling only, further runs complete) green on the stunt node.

**snapprune.1** `[done 2026-10-05]` **(bug on a shipped feature, found by the snapx.1 review and confirmed live 2026-10-05; operator: "add the prune fix — make sure the bug doesn't leave behind droppings and results in correct data retention state after the fix")** As a user with a recursive snapshot schedule, I want retention to apply to every dataset the schedule snapshots, so child datasets do not accumulate snapshots forever. **Found:** `pruneSnapshots` (`snapshot-schedules.ts`) inventories and destroys on the TARGET only; a recursive schedule takes `-r` across the subtree, so every child keeps every snapshot the schedule ever took (live: retention hourly:1, three runs → target 1, child 3). Every recursive schedule since Epic 17 shipped has been doing this. **Fix:** prune walks the schedule's SWEEP SET — the same expansion the take uses (target + descendants minus excluded subtrees; for a plain recursive schedule, the full subtree) — and applies the retention plan PER DATASET: inventory that dataset's schedule-bucket snapshots, plan, re-check `userrefs`, destroy one by one; a held child snapshot is skipped and reported like a held target snapshot, never a failure. No `destroy -r` (one held child would fail the whole verb). **Droppings:** (1) children of a plain recursive schedule converge on the first run after upgrade — the per-dataset plan keeps the policy's count and destroys the rest; (2) a dataset (and its subtree) that is now EXCLUDED but still carries this schedule's bucket snapshots from before the exclude: those are out of the schedule's scope and are destroyed outright on the next run — UNLESS another enabled schedule's sweep covers that dataset with the same bucket, in which case they are left to that schedule's retention (the schedule list is on disk; check it, don't guess). **Rulings during the build (2026-10-05):** a swept child is planned on this schedule's bucket only, keeping the most generous count among enabled same-bucket schedules that cover it (a pool-wide hourly must never cut a child's own longer hourly); "this schedule's" snapshots on an excluded dataset are the ones with the same name AND `createtxg` as a snapshot still in the sweep (received replication snapshots share names but not txgs and must survive); excluded PVE-owned datasets get the same cleanup of provably-own snapshots (our leftovers, not their data — operator: no droppings), with a refused destroy noted and non-fatal; known gap recorded in SCHEDULES-DESIGN (excluded only child after the target already pruned the names).
