The run's progress and the schedule detail report pruned/held counts per dataset, not one number. **Not recursive:** unchanged. **AHR:** unchanged (whole `@data`). **Proof:** unit tests on the per-dataset plan (child converges; held child skipped and reported; excluded dataset cleared; excluded dataset covered by another same-bucket schedule left alone; non-recursive untouched); live on the stunt node through the API over the pvepool fixture, extending `schedules-exclude-api.spec.ts`: pre-seed droppings by hand (`zfs snapshot -r` with older `anas-hourly-<stamp>` names on the target tree, including the excluded child), run the schedule once, assert the target and kept sibling hold exactly the retention count, the excluded child and its grandchild hold zero, and a held child snapshot survives and is reported. **Shipped (d1174f6):** 11 service tests + 2 route tests + notify test; `schedules-exclude-api.spec.ts` droppings test (seeded `-r` droppings incl. the excluded child and the PVE-owned nested storage root, one held sibling snapshot) green ×2 on the stunt node — target and sibling at the policy count, excluded subtree and the owned root at zero, the held one reported in `skippedHeld`, per-dataset counts in `datasets[]`.

**taskstatus.1** `[done 2026-10-06, f088778]` **(GitHub #72, filed 2026-10-06 on 0.4.1 by the #70 reporter while 0.4.2 was being cut — the same symptom from a European time zone; reply asks for confirmation on 0.4.2.)** **(bug on a shipped feature, operator 2026-10-06 on a production node after the day's PVE reboot: "All jobs show never run. Next run shows correct timing")** As a user looking at the Backup, Cloud Sync or Snapshot Schedules grid after the node rebooted, I want the last run and its verdict to survive the reboot, so a task that ran yesterday does not read "never run" until it fires again. **Found:** `deriveTaskStatus` (`task-units.ts`, backup + cloud) the schedule status derivation (`snapshot-schedule-units.ts` ~278) and replication's own copy (`replication-units.ts` ~381/423) take `lastRunAt` from the SERVICE unit's `ExecMainExitTimestamp` / `InactiveEnterTimestamp` and the verdict from its `Result` / `ExecMainStatus`; systemd does not persist service runtime properties across a boot, so every task reads "never run" after a reboot while the next-run column (timer, persistent) stays right. Confirmed on the node: service timestamps empty, timer `LastTriggerUSec` and the stamp file intact, the runner's JSON result line in the journal. **Fix (one shared derivation, used by all four: backup, cloud, schedules, replication):** when the service carries no exit timestamp and is not active, take the last run from the journal's last runner result line (`{"task":…,"result":{"status":…}}` — `readLastSuccessAt` already scans for it; generalise to "last result of any status", returning status, timestamp and the cancelled reason) and set the verdict from it; if the journal holds no result line (rotated, or the unit ran under a build that printed none), fall back to the timer's `LastTriggerUSec` with `lastRunResult: 'unknown'` and a `lastRunNote` "ran at <time>; result not retained across the reboot" (honest, not a fake success). Live service properties win whenever present (a run since boot). `lastSuccessAt` / overdue keep their current journal path. **Proof:** unit tests with injected properties for the three shapes (live props; empty props + journal result; empty props + no journal line + timer stamp) in the task-units and schedule-units suites; live on the stunt node: a backup task, a snapshot schedule and a replication task each run once (replication at least by unit test if no local-target replication fixture is cheap), the node is rebooted, and GET status shows the pre-reboot last run and verdict for both, with the API spec asserting it. **Not in scope:** persisting anything ourselves (stateless), changing what the runner prints. **Shipped:** the whole derivation extracted into `unit-run-status.ts` (operator: "no reason for this kind of bug to be distributed around"); backup/cloud, schedules and replication are thin callers keeping only their kind-specific layers (result map + cadence overdue; exit code only from the live rung; ZFS lag). A rung-2 guard: a timer stamp newer than the last journal result line by >5 s falls to the timer rung (a failed run prints no result line). Residual: a failed manual Run-Now followed by a reboot shows the earlier success. Proof: 4-rung unit suite + one wiring test per caller; `task-status-reboot.spec.ts` (schedule via its unit + a cloud task, node rebooted, status identical after) green ×2; backup by unit test (no PBS on the stunt node).


**Batch D — identity fixes that drive destructive actions (operator 2026-10-06, from `docs/IDENTITY-AUDIT-2026-10-06.md`: "the most critical ident bugs that drive destructive actions, and related changes needed to clean those").** Four stories on disjoint files; each bug is on a shipped feature; each destructive path gets a live proof on the stunt node (sequenced-mutation rule) before the tag.

**GLM review of batch D (2026-10-06):** no unconditional data loss; ten findings, six fixed before the tag as batch E: replication hold release is all-or-nothing per side and never on an unreadable task list (a failed hold on the new base must not release the old one); the datasets router never falls through to a snapshot route on a read failure (a transient `zfs list` error had reverted `tank/snapshots/old` to snapshot destroy); the source guard discloses missing ZFS facts and refuses rather than silently losing the unmounted-dataset protection; recycle containment anchored at the share path; the UI confirm resend re-prompts on a fresh code (IDENTITY_MISMATCH) instead of alerting; untested gate failure paths covered. Recorded as intended: a deleted or disabled schedule's stamped snapshots are kept (the delete dialog already says so) and a schedule re-created under the same id adopts them (ids are user-supplied); `blkid` failing on a file vdev skips the file (fail-closed); a legacy-named cred file of a line that names no file survives edit (identity epic).

**ident.1** `[done 2026-10-06, 941b6ad + 56f6505]` **ZFS: ownership stamping + stable-id confirms + wipe-by-identity + reserved names (audit #1, #2, #11, #12, #20).** (a) Every scheduled ZFS snapshot is taken with `-o anas:schedule=<id>` (one atomic verb, same call); prune destroys only snapshots carrying THIS schedule's stamp; an unstamped `anas-<bucket>-*` snapshot (pre-0.4.2 take) is prunable only when exactly one enabled schedule targets or sweeps that dataset, else left with a per-dataset note; a bucket absent from the policy is left alone (no longer "keep 0") — the stamp makes the cross-schedule collision impossible and the absent-bucket rule stops a "minimal" preset from erasing a sibling schedule's hourlies; received snapshots carry the sender's stamp and are never this schedule's. (b) One helper in `safety/` binds a stable id into the confirm params and re-reads it in the job: pool destroy/export bind the pool guid (`zpool get guid`), dataset destroy binds the dataset guid and re-runs `ownedDescendant` + the LUN-held check in the job, rollback binds the target snapshot's createtxg AND the newest snapshot's createtxg (a newer snapshot since the challenge = refuse, re-challenge); mismatch = 409 with the reason, never a silent act on a namesake. (c) Pool-destroy disk cleanup: a leaf path that is not a kernel path is used as given (never the stripped `by-id` whole disk); non-ONLINE leaves are skipped; before any `wipefs`/`labelclear`/`zap` the leaf's `blkid -p -s UUID` must equal the pool guid captured before the destroy. (d) `DatasetPath` reserves the sub-resource segments `snapshots`, `access`, `permissions` at create and rename (400), and the datasets router treats a path that names an EXISTING dataset as a dataset, never as a snapshot sub-route. (e) Recursive chown/chmod/setfacl refuse when `ownedDescendant` is non-null. **Proof:** unit tests per rule; live: a schedule take shows the property, two schedules on one dataset keep each other's buckets, a renamed-away-and-recreated pool refuses the queued destroy with 409, a by-partuuid leaf pool destroy with cleanup wipes only its leaves (loop devices), a dataset named `tank/snapshots/x` is destroyed as a dataset. **Shipped:** `ident-zfs.spec.ts` 5/5 live (stamp with source local on target and child; standard + minimal schedules on one dataset keep each other's buckets; a pool swapped under the same name → 409 IDENTITY_MISMATCH naming both guids; `<pool>/snapshots/x` destroyed as a dataset, `<pool>@x` survives, new reserved name 400; by-partuuid leaf cleanup wipes only its partition); `schedules-exclude-api.spec.ts` 7/7 with stamped droppings and a `zfs send -p` copy read as `received` and left. Judgement calls recorded: own cadence bucket always planned (0 when absent); stamped snapshots still pruned under an incomplete schedule list; in-job schedule re-read deferred to the identity epic.

**ident.2** `[done 2026-10-06, b633ce4]` **iSCSI + image restore: the served device is the identity (audit #8, #9, #10, #14).** (a) Before any zfs verb on a LUN's backing (resize, destroyBacking, repair) the served backstore's major:minor (configfs) must equal `stat(/dev/zvol/<ds>).rdev`; mismatch = 409 "LUN serves a different device than its path names". (b) LUN delete and resize confirms bind `{target, serial, backstore}`; the job re-reads the LUN under `withIscsiLock` and refuses on mismatch (index reuse can never hit a new LUN). (c) Whole-image restore: under the lock the job re-reads the LUN and requires the same serial, backing path, rdev and size; the confirm binds `{serial, snapshot, archive}`. (d) PBS backup group: creating a task refuses a `(repo, namespace, backupId)` already used by another task (409 naming it); new tasks default `backupId` to `<host>-<task>`; existing tasks keep theirs. **Proof:** unit tests; live on the iSCSI fixture: rename-and-recreate a zvol under a live LUN → resize and delete refuse; delete+add within the confirm TTL → resend refuses; restore to a re-created LUN refuses. **Shipped:** `ident-iscsi.spec.ts` 3/3 live (rename-and-recreate under a live LUN → grow and destroyBacking delete 409 served-device-mismatch, plain delete allowed; delete+add within the TTL → stale code refused with a fresh challenge; stale restore code after re-create refused, restore after rename-and-recreate 409, served volume keeps its marker). Resize has no confirm by design (grow is non-destructive); repair can only refuse a path another live backstore serves.

**ident.3** `[done 2026-10-06, baa6353]` **AHR: destructive verbs act only on members whose on-disk identity is ours (audit #3, #4, #5, #6, #7).** (a) Create and spare-attach re-run `collectDisks`/`isComposableDisk` inside the job before the first destructive step; a disk enters `planned` only after its own wipe succeeds, so rollback never zaps a disk we never touched. (b) Destroy, cache detach and the boot recovery ladder act only on partitions whose `mdadm --examine --export` UUID is pinned in mdadm.conf (or live for this pool) and on cache slices that are PVs of this pool's VG UUID; the ladder never `--run`s an unpinned array; create refuses a pool name that already has arrays, labels or pins. (c) Spare removal acts only on members mdstat flags `(S)` at job time; an active member = 409. (d) Destroy clears the expansion intent and returns 409 while any `ahr.*` job or an intent run is in flight; the intent records the md/VG UUIDs and a mismatch reads as stale (never "halted"); resume re-checks each disk and goes through a confirm. **Proof:** unit tests; live on loop devices (the self-heal rig): a foreign `media-r1` array on the node survives a pool destroy and a reboot-ladder run; a spare that took over a rebuild cannot be removed; destroy-then-recreate under the same name shows no stale intent. **Shipped:** `test/stunt-node/ident3-gt.sh run` 39/39 live on loop devices + hot7–9 (a spare that took over a rebuild → 409 on removal and left in the array; a foreign unpinned `media-r1` ghost — built by INCREMENTAL assembly, the shape udev leaves at boot — stays inactive across an anasd restart with the scan logging `action=ignored reason=not-pinned-not-in-vg`; the same foreign array survives DELETE /v1/ahr/media with its UUID, superblocks and data byte-identical, reported as `foreignArrays`; create refuses the occupied name before any confirm; a halted intent is removed by destroy; after recreate under the same name no expansion, a planted stale record reads stale, resume 409). Rig lessons (test infra): an explicit `--assemble --no-degraded` of a lone member creates no array and a plain assemble starts a RAID1 member degraded; the ghost must be addressed by its kernel device (the md/ link is not created for an inactive array); holding the udev exec queue hangs the destroy (partition events). Also on the node: the leftover `lpahr` fixture pool from an earlier rig was removed through the gated destroy (arrays stopped, pins and labels cleared) as an unplanned live exercise.

**ident.4** `[done 2026-10-06, 0a32e87]` **Mounts, replication, cloud, recycle: act on what is actually there (audit #13, #15, #16, #17, #18, #19).** (a) Replication hold tag is per task (`anas-repl-<hash(location,target)>`); a run releases only its own tag; the existing global tag is migrated on the task's next run (release global, take own) so no base is ever unheld in between. (b) Cloud remote PUT that changes the destination (type, endpoint, bucket, path, host) is a 409 + confirm naming every task that references the remote, through wrappers. (c) The source guard realpaths the source and adds ZFS `mountpoint,canmount,mounted` facts: an unmounted dataset or a target whose live source differs from the spec is "not mounted" (refused), not an empty success. (d) Mount delete removes a credentials file only when it lives under the ANAS creds dir and no other fstab line references it; the generated name is hashed from the full mountpoint so two mountpoints can never share one. (e) Mount remove/unmount/disable/edit `umount` only when the live source and fstype at the target match the fstab spec; otherwise 409 naming what is mounted there. (f) Recycle purge `lstat`s the bin, refuses a symlink, requires a single-segment repository name and checks every file's realpath stays under the bin. **Proof:** unit tests; live: two replication tasks on one source keep both bases; a local fs mounted at a failed CIFS target survives Remove; a symlinked `#recycle` is skipped with a note. **Shipped:** `ident-mounts-repl.spec.ts` 4/4 live (two tasks on one source keep both bases under their own tags; a loop-backed ext4 at a failed CIFS target survives Remove with a 409 naming it; remote PUT changing the endpoint → 409 naming the task; symlinked `#recycle` skipped with the note). Spec helper fixed during the run: Run-now starts the unit `--no-block` and answers `{started:true}`, completion = the runner's result line in the unit journal.
